To address this, we introduce Vision2Code, a multi-domain, reference-code-free benchmark containing 539 test examples from 15 different datasets, spanning charts and plots, geometry, graphs, scientific diagrams, documents and 3D spatial scenes. Evaluation is done by executing the generated programs and comparing their rendered outputs to the source images using dataset- and domain-specific rubrics for a VLM judge. We find that the final scores obtained in this manner have substantially higher correlation with human ratings than those obtained via generic VLM rubrics or standard pixel-based, embedding-based or perceptual similarity metrics. We further demonstrate the reliability of our evaluation under repeated sampling from the VLM judges, varying rubric weights, and in the presence of controlled errors. Our results highlight the variability across domains of existing image-to-code generation models, with accuracy on charts and graphs being significantly higher than other domains. Lastly, we demonstrate the utility of our scoring method for rejection sampling of generated programs for fine-tuning, improving Qwen3.5-9B by 16% and Phi-4-MM by 28% compared to their base checkpoints. Our code and data are publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[OccludeBench: Can VLMs Find What's Missing?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13790)**  `arXiv:2610.13790`  `cs.CV` `cs.CL`  
  _Jasin Cekinmez, Addison J. Wu, Ryo Mitsuhashi, Linrong Cai, Xingyu Fu, Zhuang Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  How well can vision-language models (VLMs) reason about what is missing from an image? We study this question with OccludeBench, a manually curated benchmark for recovering fully occluded objects from the visual evidence that remains. OccludeBench distinguishes direct cases, where the hidden object must be recovered from a specific physical cue such as a shadow or reflection, from inferential cases, where it can be inferred from broader scene context. Across five VLMs, accuracy averages 42.7% on direct cases versus 62.7% on inferential cases, exhibiting a 20.0-point gap. Humans attain higher accuracies of 63.4% versus 68.5% for direct and inferential images, respectively, with no significant gap. To locate this failure, we introduce OccludeTrace, a four-stage diagnostic that isolates where the visual reasoning process breaks down. The bottleneck emerges when models must recover the relevant object from the projection within the full image: they succeed on only 50% of failed examples, compared with 91% of examples they answer correctly. Extended reasoning, tool use, and few-shot prompting yield inconsistent improvements and do not reliably eliminate the gap. In contrast, an inpaint-then-confront intervention, which prompts models to first generate a candidate hidden object and then evaluate that hypothesis against the visible physical cue, reduces the gap to a statistically non-significant difference for both models with image-generation capabilities. Together, our results establish a tractable, controlled diagnostic for isolating how VLMs reason about visual content that is missing from an image.
  </details>

- **[MVGraph: Benchmarking LVLMs on Evidence Composition across Multi-View Visual Graph](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12961)**  `arXiv:2610.12961`  `cs.CV` `cs.CL`  
  _Yingjie Zhu, Han Cheng, Xuefeng Bai, Youcheng Pan, Yang Xiang, Kehai Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We introduce MVGraph, a benchmark for evaluating large vision-language models (LVLMs) on multi-graph reasoning where the evidence required by a query is distributed across multiple interrelated visual graph views. Although such evidence commonly resides in source-specific graphs, temporal snapshots, or local observations, existing graph reasoning benchmarks remain confined to a single self-contained structure, assessing only graph perception and reasoning while leaving the alignment and composition of evidence across views underexplored. MVGraph comprises 8k instances spanning 8 \textit{graph-theoretic} tasks, abstracted from two multi-graph scenarios (partial and temporal graph views). Each problem is programmatically constructed and verified to be unsolvable from a single view. Beyond end-to-end evaluation, we decompose multi-graph reasoning into four complementary capabilities---Perception, Alignment, Composition, and Reasoning---and design 29 diagnostic tasks with 64k instances to probe each one, along with golden intermediate representations for evaluating the intermediate process. Across 15 LVLMs, we reveal the difficulty of multi-graph reasoning and identify cross-view edge alignment and relation-level composition as prominent bottlenecks through stage-oriented diagnostics. Meanwhile, temporal constraints and increasing view counts expose further capability boundaries. Code and data are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Video2World: Benchmarking Coding Agents for Interactive World Modeling from Embodied Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04432)**  `arXiv:2610.04432`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.RO`  
  _Jinzhou Tang, Zijun Zhang, Jing Yang, Yuchen Yan, Kun Zhou, Lingjun Mao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Building interactive simulators from real-world observations is a promising way to scale embodied data, but current pipelines still rely heavily on manual environment construction and calibration. We study whether frontier foundation models and coding agents can automate this process end to end. We formulate \emph{autonomous video-to-simulation} as a software engineering task in which an agent observes an embodied video, constructs the corresponding simulated environment and robot behavior, and iteratively refines the result through execution feedback. To evaluate this capability, we introduce \textbf{Video2World}, a benchmark comprising 222 reconstruction instances derived from 189 robot and human demonstration videos. Video2World measures reconstructed worlds along geometric fidelity, dynamic fidelity, and functional correctness, capturing spatial perception, physical reasoning, and executable interaction. Evaluating 9 frontier coding-agent systems reveals a sharp improvement in Task success beginning with Claude Opus 5, rising from below 5\% to over 15\%, while substantial gaps to human-assisted reconstruction remain. We further find that worlds that look better could work worse: better visual fidelity does not always lead to higher task success. This echoes the broader gap between perceptual realism and factual correctness observed in generative models.
  </details>

- **[SpatialASM: Internalizing Active Mentalization for Spatial Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13832)**  `arXiv:2610.13832`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Yuhong Liu, Shenxi Wu, Junyi Chi, Beichen Zhang, Xuanlang Dai, Jiaqi Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  When solving complex spatial reasoning tasks, ordinary humans naturally revisit the scenes from task-relevant viewpoints and mentally reconstruct the spatial layouts from those viewpoints. But can Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) proactively think in the same way? We term this ability Active Spatial Mentalization (ASM), an intrinsic component of visual intelligence that remains substantially underdeveloped in current LVLMs. To bridge this gap, we introduce SpatialASM, a scalable framework to internalize ASM into LVLMs through training-time spatial simulation. SpatialASM employs an allocentric novel-view generator to ground hypothetical viewpoints in visual evidence and produces two complementary forms of supervision: i) explicit ASM trajectories that teach LVLMs when and how to adaptively invoke view planning and imagination during spatial reasoning, and ii) automatically-constructed perspective-taking tasks that further strengthens viewpoint-conditioned scene imagination. On seven benchmarks spanning relatively perception-oriented spatial understanding to more challenging tasks requiring multi-image, cross-view and viewpoint-transformation reasoning, SpatialASM yields performance gain on LVLMs of four LVLM backbones in spatial reasoning while retaining their original general vision capabilities. The experimental results demonstrate that our approach improves average accuracy across the seven spatial benchmarks. And the synthesis of ASM trajectories provides a scalable pathway to alleviate the scarcity of spatial Chain-of-Thought (CoT) supervision for eliciting active viewpoint exploration and mental scene simulation.
  </details>

- **[When MLLMs miss what humans read: diagnosing and learning to read squint text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13465)**  `arXiv:2610.13465`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Chen Peng, Shan Huang, Jianbo Huang, Linrui Qin, Qibo Sun, Mengwei Xu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal LLMs can fail to read text embedded in an image's coarse structure (text a human recovers by squinting) and report that no text is present. We name this class Squint Text and study it as a recognition deficiency, motivated by cases from a production moderation pipeline. We introduce SquintBench, comprising 1076 production-derived moderation images, and SquintCtrl, a controlled synthetic suite for mechanism diagnosis. Frozen-weight analysis shows that hidden text leaves a coarse signal while glyph identity is weakly represented; identity becomes substantially more accessible when the correct spatial regions are supplied, implicating spatial selection as a major bottleneck. Suppressing high-frequency content also improves reading at a fixed visual-token budget, while visual token count may also contribute. These findings motivate Squint OPSD: a frozen teacher receives a downscaled view and the ground-truth transcription as privileged context, and its token-level distributions supervise a student along on-policy trajectories. At inference, the student reads the original image alone. Squint OPSD reaches 88.61 ANLS on SquintBench, versus 26.50 for the unadapted backbone, 24.44 for the best general-purpose model on the original view, and 68.60 at best for an off-the-shelf model under a downscaled view. These results substantially narrow the gap between available visual evidence and its generated read-out without auxiliary views at inference.
  </details>

- **[Rethinking Contrastive Loss in CLIP Post-training: A Complementary Framework with Frozen Text Encoder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11374)**  `arXiv:2610.11374`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Zidan Wang, Yaqian Li, Xiaokai Zhang, Kaiwen Long, Kun He, Hanpeng Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  CLIP serves as a foundational vision-language model and the de facto vision encoder for downstream VLMs such as LLaVA. Post-training offers a lightweight route to refine CLIP, but recent work argues that the standard contrastive loss is unsuitable for post-training due to catastrophic forgetting under small batches, motivating designs that abandon the contrastive objective in favor of distillation. We revisit this premise and find that, for the InfoNCE objective, the reported forgetting is driven primarily not by insufficient negatives but by an inappropriate magnitude of the contrastive temperature $\tau$: with $\tau$ set sufficiently small, contrastive post-training improves rather than degrades the pretrained CLIP, which we explain through the temperature dependence of the InfoNCE gradient. Building on this finding, we propose \textbf{ComCLIP}, a lightweight single-epoch post-training recipe that freezes CLIP's text encoder---so the refined vision encoder is a drop-in replacement with unchanged architecture and inference cost---and trains the vision encoder with a properly-tempered contrastive loss, an MSE anchoring loss against the original CLIP, and a relational distillation loss from DINOv2. Over multiple seeds, ComCLIP matches the self-distillation baseline CLIP-Refine on zero-shot classification while significantly improving the transferability of visual features, measured by linear probing ($48.99$ vs.\ $42.28$ on ViT-B/16), and on ViT-L/14 it also improves MMVP over CLIP-Refine ($24.20$ vs.\ $19.01$); CLIP-Refine remains stronger on image-text retrieval. Used as a drop-in vision encoder for LLaVA-1.5-7B without re-aligning the projector or LLM, ComCLIP yields no net change across $8$ VLM benchmarks, i.e., the refinement does not break downstream compatibility. Code and models are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[AgroGround: Multi-Granularity Grounded Recognition in Agriculture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04425)**  `arXiv:2610.04425`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Abdulla Alshehhi, Zongyan Han, Rao Muhammad Anwer_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Agricultural visual models are typically evaluated for either recognition or localization, but reliable diagnosis requires identifying what is present and localizing the evidence. Agricultural visual question answering (VQA) datasets carry rich semantic labels but rarely link them to image regions, and adding such annotations by hand is costly at scale. We introduce AgroGround, a large-scale dataset for grounded agricultural recognition: identifying plant diseases and other agricultural targets and localizing their image regions. An automated pipeline converts the labels of eight agricultural VQA datasets into annotations for disease lesions and whole objects, producing 794,850 instruction examples. Healthy images provide negative supervision for disease queries, teaching the model to return empty predictions. We fine-tune a shared vision-language model on known-target grounding instructions combined with instructions requiring both recognition and localization. We evaluate predicted identities, regions, joint correctness, and healthy-image abstention on 1,480 human-verified images disjoint from all training data. Grounding-only fine-tuning reduces recognition accuracy from 51.8\% to 29.1\%, while adding recognition-and-localization instructions raises it to 72.6\%. With images and annotations held fixed, combining the two formats raises joint accuracy from 19.2\% to 43.3\% at comparable grounding. Healthy negatives raise abstention on healthy images to 95.0\%, and reinforcement learning improves lesion-level grounding. The resulting 2B model exceeds its annotation teacher in grounding F1 on our benchmark and on the external PlantSeg test set. AgroGround establishes a benchmark for grounded agricultural recognition, measuring joint correctness of identity and localization along with abstention on healthy images. The code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[TDDN: Text-aligned Diffused DINO Network for Puzzle Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07937)**  `arXiv:2609.07937`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Harsha Patnala, Debopriyo Banerjee, Ayush Sunil Munot, Somak Aditya_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Structured visual reasoning, such as image puzzles, demands fine-grained visual perception, which current Vision Language Models (VLMs) often lack. VLMs built on CLIP-based ViT backbones tend to trade fine-grained visual details for high-level semantics. We show that this deficit propagates downstream, affecting various vision and language tasks. To address this, we fuse DINOv3 and CleanDIFT representations into a perception encoder (DiffusedDINO), and align it with RoBERTa-L, yielding a text-aligned model (TDDN) that preserves this perceptual advantage: with frozen backbones and only ~590K alignment pairs, TDDN surpasses CLIP ViT-L/14 in image-text retrieval tasks: text-to-image on Flickr30K and both directions on MS-COCO-2014. TDDN more than triples CLIP's dense-prediction accuracy (ADE20K 5.20 -> 18.11 mIoU, COCO-Stuff 7.35 -> 24.44), despite CLIP's massive training corpus. TDDN leads on segmentation benchmarks among general-purpose contrastive encoders, including SigLIP 2. We further introduce PuzzlePerception, a segmentation and visual question answering dataset that probes fine-grained spatial understanding, on which TDDN doubles CLIP's segmentation accuracy (11.04 -> 22.51 mIoU). Our code and data are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[VITAL: Visual-Semantic Dual Supervision for Enhanced and Interpretable Latent Reasoning in Medical MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28422)**  `arXiv:2605.28422`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Qiaoru Li, Shaotian Liang, Jintao Chen, Haoran Sun, Yuxiang Cai, Jianwei Yin, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Latent reasoning enables reasoning over continuous hidden states rather than explicit tokens, avoiding the language bottleneck and inference overhead of chain-of-thought for medical VQA. However, existing methods suffer from modality collapse, insufficient visual supervision, and train-inference mismatch. Moreover, their opaque latent states offer no interpretability, which is critical in clinical applications. We propose VITAL, a latent-space reasoning framework for medical MLLMs with visual-semantic dual supervision: an auxiliary text decoder reconstructs reasoning chains from latent states, while a visual projector regresses ROI features from a frozen, independent medical vision encoder. Both modules are discarded at inference with zero overhead, yet can be re-attached post-hoc for dual interpretability, providing textual and visual explanations of the reasoning process without sacrificing efficiency. We construct a 61K dataset spanning 9 imaging modalities, exceeding prior medical visual latent reasoning datasets by an order of magnitude. Experiments on 7 benchmarks show that VITAL consistently and substantially outperforms the backbone, all latent reasoning baselines, and medical MLLMs trained on far larger data, achieving state-of-the-art results competitive with trillion-parameter proprietary models.
  </details>

- **[LensVLM: Selective Context Expansion for Compressed Visual Representation of Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07019)**  `arXiv:2605.07019`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Roy Xie, Dan Friedman, Donghan Yu, Bowen Pan, Christopher Fifty, Jang-Hyun Kim, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision Language Models (VLMs) offer the exciting possibility of processing text as rendered images, bypassing the need for tokenizing the text into long token sequences. Since VLM image encoders map fixed-size images to a fixed number of visual tokens, varying rendering resolution provides a fine-grained compression knob. However, accuracy deteriorates quickly as compression increases: characters shrink below the vision encoder's effective resolution, making them indistinguishable. To address this, we propose LensVLM, an inference framework and post-training recipe that enables VLMs to scan compressed images, then selectively expand only the relevant images to their uncompressed form via learned tools. Building on Qwen3.5-9B-Base, LensVLM maintains accuracy comparable to the full-text upper bound at 4.3$\times$ effective compression and outperforms retrieval-based, text- and visual-compression baselines up to 10.1$\times$ effective compression across seven text QA benchmarks. LensVLM also generalizes to multimodal document and code understanding tasks, with the accuracy gain over baselines growing as compression increases. Our analysis validates this approach: training makes visual compression robust to rendering choices, and as compression grows the model increasingly relies on expanded content rather than unreliable visual reading. The analysis also yields practical tool-choice guidance: text expansion is preferable for rendered text, while high-resolution image expansion suits native documents whose layout cues carry task-relevant information.
  </details>

- **[CORE-Seg: Reasoning-Driven Segmentation for Complex Lesions via Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05911)**  `arXiv:2603.05911`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Yuxin Xie, Yuming Chen, Yishan Yang, Yi Zhou, Tao Zhou, Zhen Zhao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Medical image segmentation is undergoing a paradigm shift from conventional visual pattern matching to cognitive reasoning analysis. Although Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have shown promise in integrating linguistic and visual knowledge, significant gaps remain: existing general MLLMs possess broad common sense but lack the specialized visual reasoning required for complex lesions, whereas traditional segmentation models excel at pixel-level segmentation but lack logical interpretability. In this paper, we introduce ComLesion-14K, the first diverse Chain-of-Thought (CoT) benchmark for reasoning-driven complex lesion segmentation. To accomplish this task, we propose CORE-Seg, an end-to-end framework integrating reasoning with segmentation through a Semantic-Guided Prompt Adapter. We design a progressive training strategy from SFT to GRPO, equipped with an adaptive dual-granularity reward mechanism to mitigate reward sparsity. Our Method achieves state-of-the-art results with a mean Dice of 37.06\% (14.89\% higher than the second-best baseline), while reducing the failure rate to 18.42\%. Project Page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[ITO: Multi-View Alignment and Training-Time Fusion for Image-Text Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02767)**  `arXiv:2603.02767`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Hanpeng Liu, Yaqian Li, Zidan Wang, Shuoxi Zhang, Zonglin Zhao, Zihao Bo, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image--text contrastive pretraining has become a dominant paradigm for visual representation learning, yet existing methods often yield representations that remain partially organized by modality rather than by semantics. We propose ITO, a framework addressing this limitation through two complementary mechanisms with distinct roles. Multimodal multiple alignment enriches supervision by constructing diverse cross-modal correspondences from multi-view image augmentations, providing the primary source of discriminative gain. A lightweight training-time multimodal fusion module then acts as a training-time regularizer, encouraging the encoders to produce features that are compatible under fusion. Crucially, the fusion module is discarded at inference, preserving the efficiency of standard dual-encoder architectures while incurring training-time overhead only. Extensive experiments across pretraining scales from millions to billions of image--text pairs show that ITO outperforms strong contrastive baselines at moderate scale and yields consistent gains over CLIP under identical data, backbone, and compute at the 100M--1B scale, on classification, retrieval, and multimodal benchmarks. Controlled comparisons show that these gains are not explained by additional training compute or image augmentation alone. Our analysis further reveals that the contribution of fusion grows with data scale and that it stabilizes optimization, mitigating the late-stage overfitting commonly observed in aggressive contrastive learning. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[WoundAmbit: Bridging State-of-the-Art Semantic Segmentation and Real-World Wound Care](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06185)**  `arXiv:2504.06185`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Vanessa Borst, Timo Dittus, Tassilo Dege, Astrid Schmieder, Samuel Kounev_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Chronic wounds affect a large population, particularly the elderly and diabetic patients, who often exhibit limited mobility and co-existing health conditions. Automated wound monitoring via mobile image capture can reduce in-person physician visits by enabling remote tracking of wound size. Semantic segmentation is key to this process, yet wound segmentation remains underrepresented in medical imaging research. To address this, we benchmark state-of-the-art deep learning models from general-purpose vision, medical imaging, and top methods from public wound challenges. For a fair comparison, we standardize training, data augmentation, and evaluation, conducting cross-validation to minimize partitioning bias. We also assess real-world deployment aspects, including generalization to an out-of-distribution wound dataset, computational efficiency, and interpretability. Additionally, we propose a reference object-based approach to convert AI-generated masks into clinically relevant wound size estimates and evaluate this, along with mask quality, for the five best architectures based on physician assessments. Overall, the transformer-based TransNeXt showed the highest levels of generalizability. Despite variations in inference times, all models processed at least one image per second on the CPU, which is deemed adequate for the intended application. Interpretability analysis typically revealed prominent activations in wound regions, emphasizing focus on clinically relevant features. Expert evaluation showed high mask approval for all analyzed models, with VWFormer and ConvNeXtS backbone performing the best. Size retrieval accuracy was similar across models, and predictions closely matched expert annotations. Finally, we demonstrate how our AI-driven wound size estimation framework, WoundAmbit, is integrated into a custom telehealth system.
  </details>

- **[Towards Physically Grounded Articulated 3D Assets: A Multi-Agent Pipeline and Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13947)**  `arXiv:2610.13947`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xudong Cai, Ruijie Zhu, Haoliang Shang, Ruining Li, Marc Pollefeys, Shangzhe Wu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In this paper, we study physically grounded articulated 3D asset generation---moving the goal from reproducing how objects look to capturing how they move and function. Inspired by the literature on procedural and agentic generation, we present Artisan, a system that extends the latest method Articraft by introducing a multi-agent pipeline with physical simulation feedback, enabling comprehensive attributes beyond geometry, appearance, and physically plausible articulation, such as collision-free motion, stability under gravity, and accurate mass and material properties. Furthermore, we construct a unified benchmark spanning four key aspects: spatial fidelity, physical plausibility, downstream task performance, and overall quality. The benchmark encompasses 100 reference assets from ArtVIP and LightWheel, 50 stability prompts, and 50 ABO objects with reference masses, with protocols evaluating various metrics. Through extensive evaluation of more than 10 representative models, Artisan achieves competitive spatial fidelity while attaining the lowest collision rates and highest stability rates among the evaluated methods. Together, our system and benchmark establish insights for advancing 3D generation towards physically plausible, functionally usable assets.
  </details>

- **[Images Talk Back: Guiding MMDiTs by Suppressing Image-to-Text Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13941)**  `arXiv:2610.13941`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yucheng Yang, Xiaoxiao Sun, Shusheng Yang, Jiakang Chen, Serena Yeung-Levy_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal diffusion transformers (MMDiTs) allow the evolving image to update internal text representations through joint attention. These representations then guide subsequent image updates, raising a question: does this feedback help correct deviations from the prompt, or preserve them? We find that image-to-text (I2T) feedback can preserve the objects and attributes already represented in the evolving image, including errors. Transplanting I2T messages can rescue failures or disrupt successes. At matched intervention strengths, suppressing the influence of I2T feedback improves generation, whereas opposite controls degrade it. These findings motivate Semantic Feedback Guidance (SFG), which suppresses the influence of I2T feedback by guiding away from an auxiliary branch that strengthens this feedback and weakens text-to-image conditioning. The in-place variant, iSFG, directly suppresses I2T feedback and strengthens text-to-image conditioning, requiring no extra model evaluations. Temporal interventions motivate running the SFG branch during the first 20% of image sampling steps, requiring about $1.2\times$ the base NFE without classifier-free guidance (CFG). Experiments across three image-generation backbones show gains in alignment and preference-model scores. On FLUX, adding SFG to CFG raises GenEval from $0.6661$ to $0.7479$; on SD3.5M, iSFG adds $+1.33$ HPSv2.1 over CFG at zero extra NFE. The control also transfers to text-to-video and image-to-video generation, improving VBench scores. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Neurons++: A Unified Visual-Stream Framework for Image and Video Reconstruction from fMRI with Neural-to-Visual Interpretability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13763)**  `arXiv:2610.13763`  `cs.CV`  
  _Haonan Wang, Yuchen Wang, Jingyu Lu, Qixiang Zhang, Lehan Wang, Xuanqi Huang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Decoding visual experience from fMRI is important for understanding human perception and developing brain-computer interfaces. Recent generative decoding methods can reconstruct plausible images and videos, but visual plausibility alone does not ensure that the output is faithfully grounded in brain activity. In this work, we propose Neurons++, a visual-stream fMRI reconstruction framework for both static image and dynamic video reconstruction. Compared with our conference version Neurons, Neurons++ extends the paradigm from video-only reconstruction to visual-stream reconstruction for both images and videos, and replaces parallel task conditioning with serial cortex-inspired decoding. The model first uses image-fMRI pretraining to obtain a stable spatial-semantic initialization, and then performs subject-specific fine-tuning for image and video reconstruction separately. The serial design organizes fMRI-derived representations into explicit spatial, semantic, language-level, and temporal cues. Extensive experiments show that Neurons++ achieves state-of-the-art video reconstruction and competitive image reconstruction across standard benchmarks. Beyond reconstruction fidelity, neural-to-visual analyses indicate that Neurons++ exhibits qualitative directional controllability under neural perturbation, that its fMRI encoder read-out is sensitive to stimulus-relevant cortical features, and that it supports ROI-guided semantic modulation. Code and model weights are available at:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Gaussian Splatting with Reflections Guided by What is Seen](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13757)**  `arXiv:2610.13757`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yiming Liang, Tianyu Xiao, Hiroshi Ishikawa_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While novel view synthesis methods like Neural Radiance Fields (NeRF) and 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) have shown impressive performance on common scenes, they often struggle with capturing view-dependent specular effects, particularly near-field reflections. Existing methods utilize environment maps, which only represent lights from infinity, or environment Gaussians, which rely solely on indirect supervision from reflections, are insufficient for accurately modeling such effects. Here, we propose a framework based on 2D Gaussian Splatting (2DGS) that leverages directly observable environment information in input images. Our approach also integrates a hybrid reflection appearance representation and view-dependent specular modeling. Experiments on public benchmarks and a newly curated near-field reflection dataset demonstrate that our method achieves superior rendering quality and geometric accuracy.
  </details>

- **[State-Isolated Vision Mamba via Update-Tendency Routing for Underwater Image Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13720)**  `arXiv:2610.13720`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yan Luo, Laibin Chang, Zefan Feng, Yi'ang Chen, Kui Jiang, Zhengguo Li_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision Mamba has recently emerged as an efficient alternative to convolutional and Transformer-based architectures by modeling long-range spatial dependencies through recurrent state evolution. Existing studies primarily improve token serialization, scanning strategies, or expert routing, yet largely overlook a state level question question: how should recurrent states be organized for spatially heterogeneous visual restoration? In this paper, we show that forcing tokens with heterogeneous update behaviors to recurrently modify a shared hidden state leads to persistent cross-content interference, which we term \textbf{State Contention}. We further argue that effective recurrent modeling should organize state evolution according to token compatibility rather than token ordering. Based on this observation, we introduce \textbf{State Isolation}, a new recurrent state organization principle for Vision Mamba,tokens with compatible state-update tendencies evolve within independent recurrent state streams. To instantiate this principle, we propose \textbf{IsoMamba}, a $\Delta$-guided architecture that constructs model-intrinsic update representations, discovers update-compatible token groups through label-free clustering, and routes them to isolated Mamba experts following a \textbf{cluster--route--isolate} paradigm. In addition, we develop a dynamic channel-wise reweighting objective to compensate for wavelength-dependent reconstruction difficulty in underwater images. Extensive experiments demonstrate that IsoMamba consistently achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple underwater image enhancement benchmarks while providing cleaner hidden state responses, validating the effectiveness of state isolated recurrent modeling.
  </details>

- **[Benchmarking Answer-Grounding Consistency in Remote Sensing Visual Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13636)**  `arXiv:2610.13636`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ping Guo, Zhiqi Huang, Xinran Li, Linkun Yu, Jingyin Pei, Kamata Seiichiro_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In grounded remote sensing visual question answering, a model produces a textual answer and identifies relevant objects in the image as grounding evidence. Existing evaluations measure answer correctness and localization quality, but do not directly assess answer-grounding consistency. We propose the Answer-Evidence Support Protocol (AESP) to assess this consistency through evidence support: whether the grounding evidence supports the model's own answer. AESP applies task-specific rules to existence, counting, and comparison questions. We further construct the Grounded Remote Sensing Visual Question Answering (G-RSVQA) benchmark, containing 15,027 samples with questions, reference answers, and all related object-level annotations. Experiments on seven grounded vision--language models reveal failures of evidence support across all three question types. Up to 50.82% of correct answers in the evidence evaluation subset are not supported by the provided grounding evidence.
  </details>

- **[CUBE: Class-conditioned Unknown Boundary Evidence for Open-Set Insect Species Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13610)**  `arXiv:2610.13610`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hao Tan, Qimin Zhang, Zhe Xiao, Zekai Zuo, Wanting Liu, Jinghui Qin_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Open-set insect species recognition faces significant challenges when deployed in real-world biodiversity scenarios, particularly when encountering unknown species that are visually close to known training classes. Unlike generic out-of-distribution samples, fine-grained unknown insects are often not visually anomalous. They instead tend to appear near the decision boundary of specific known species, leading to unreliable closed-set predictions. To address these challenges, we propose \textbf{Class-conditioned Unknown Boundary Evidence (CUBE)}, a novel evidence-based framework that models unknownness around each known species rather than as a single global unknown class. Our key idea is to evaluate a test sample relative to the known species it most closely resembles, enabling local rejection for visually confusable unknown insects. CUBE learns class-conditioned boundary evidence from auxiliary species, preserves auxiliary species as shadow references during inference, and incorporates taxonomic candidate calibration to verify biological plausibility. When ecological metadata is available, we further introduce CUBE-E, which adds a lightweight location-consistency term without modifying the visual backbone. Experiments on the Open-Insect C-America benchmark show that CUBE achieves superior performance, outperforming state-of-the-art baselines with 95.8\%, 98.7\%, and 99.1\% AUROC on local, non-local, and non-moth unknown splits, respectively, while maintaining 94.9\% closed-set accuracy. With ecological metadata, CUBE-E further improves the results to 96.0\%, 99.7\%, and 99.5\% AUROC with 95.2\% closed-set accuracy.
  </details>

- **[Learning Holistic Scene Layout against View Shifts in Cross-View Geo-Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13553)**  `arXiv:2610.13553`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chao Yang, Songlian Li, Zhigang Tu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cross-view geo-localization remains challenging due to drastic appearance variations and geometric distortions caused by viewpoint changes. Existing methods primarily rely on discriminative feature learning or explicit geometric alignment, yet often overlook an important observation: humans tend to match scenes by integrating the spatial context of multiple landmarks rather than relying solely on isolated local visual cues. Inspired by this, we propose a Spatial Context Modeling (SCM) module, which traverses feature maps along four orthogonal directions and models interactions among the resulting directional representations at each spatial position. By aggregating complementary directional observations, SCM constructs richer scene layout representations. Since reliable spatial context depends on robust visual features, we introduce a High-Frequency Information Guidance (HFIG) module that extracts stable structural cues from the frequency domain. By emphasizing edges and contours that are less sensitive to appearance variations, HFIG encourages the model to learn more robust representations. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves competitive performance on standard cross-view geo-localization benchmarks while exhibiting strong robustness under more challenging scenarios with unknown orientations and limited fields of view (FoVs).
  </details>

- **[ELMA: Benchmarking Anaphoric Compositions for Long-Term Text-to-Motion Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13549)**  `arXiv:2610.13549`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shun Kato, Dixin Yang, Kotaro Amaya, Yuiga Wada, Kazumi Fukuda, Takashi Shibuya, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-guided long-term human motion generation is essential for applications such as continuously operating digital avatars. Despite recent progress on short clips, existing models struggle with multi-segment instructions that involve anaphora, where constraints established in earlier segments must be preserved over time. In this paper, we introduce a novel task of long-term motion generation from such anaphoric compositions. To mitigate this, we present ELMA, an episodic long-term motion dataset with dense segment-level annotations that preserve cross-segment dependencies. Built via a fully automated collection and filtering pipeline, ELMA extracts high-quality, continuous 30-second clips from in-the-wild videos, capturing the episodic narratives necessary for long-range state modeling. Leveraging this dataset, we fine-tune state-of-the-art motion generation models to incorporate long-range context. Extensive experiments reveal fundamental shortcomings in current models when handling context-dependent instructions. We demonstrate that ELMA enables significant improvements in both global physical realism and semantic alignment, setting a robust new baseline for long-term motion synthesis.
  </details>

- **[Auditing VLM Benchmarks from Existing Evaluation Runs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13548)**  `arXiv:2610.13548`  `cs.CV`  
  _Denis Degtiarev, Anna Gromova, Sergey Katkov, Artem Mikhailin, Safina Mukhamedshina, Albert Nasybullin, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Every evaluation run of a vision-language model (VLM) already produces per-question artifacts (predictions, judged correctness, metadata) that leaderboards reduce to a single score and then discard. We reuse those artifacts to audit the benchmark itself, and add an audit layer to VLMEvalKit: six detectors that run as post-processing over saved outputs, plus one additional text-only rerun with visual inputs removed, emitting a per-question report. The layer reports per-question findings together with aggregate audit statistics; for the headline quantities, it attaches permutation-null excess where applicable, interval estimates for proportions, and sensitivity to the model pool. Auditing MMStar, a benchmark curated specifically so that the image is indispensable, with three models, we find zero aggregate accuracy gain from the image on 20.2% of its questions. An item-matched permutation null accounts for 17.0%, leaving a +3.2 pp excess (corrected permutation p = 0.001). This excess is concentrated in the mathematics and science categories and is absent from coarse perception, a localization consistent with knowledge substituting for visual evidence; confirming that mechanism would require item-level review. A second detector flags 134 questions on which every model that answered in parseable form picked the same non-gold option, and these candidates peak in the perception categories, whereas the zero-gain excess is largest in mathematics and science & technology. Replacing the LLM judge with exact string matching changes no conclusion, and every scoring discrepancy between the two traces to one model whose free-form answers a parser cannot read. On a binary yes/no benchmark the detectors measurably fall out of calibration, a scope boundary that we measure and report. The audit layer, the prediction artifacts and the generated reports accompany this submission.
  </details>

- **[Stride: Scale-Aware Multi-Agent Evolutionary Refinement for Infographic Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13502)**  `arXiv:2610.13502`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yan Zhou, Yang Yang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Infographic generation challenges text-to-image models because a single image must satisfy numerous visual, textual, and semantic constraints. Iterative agentic refinement with a multimodal verifier is a natural remedy, but existing methods face a scale mismatch: local edits cannot correct global composition errors, while full regeneration may destroy already-correct regions. We introduce Stride, a constraint-grounded multi-agent framework that treats refinement over any frozen generator as a history-aware evolution strategy. Stride separates constraint evaluation - whether each requirement is satisfied - from visual grounding - where and how it is violated. It then routes global revisions to a composer subagent and local corrections to a retoucher subagent. Each subagent conducts internal planning and generates multiple candidates, while Stride accepts a candidate only when it improves the verifier score, reducing regression across iterations. Because stochastic generators and imperfect verifiers make any single step unreliable, it is this combination of scale-aware routing, per-step redundancy, and monotonic acceptance that yields stable improvement across iterations. With fully open-weight generator-verifier systems, Stride outperforms proprietary systems including Nano-Banana-2.0 and Seedream-5.0-Pro on BizGenEval, matches GPT-Image-2.0 and surpasses Seedream-5.0-Pro on IGenBench, and achieves a new state-of-the-art score on WISE, while substantially outperforming existing agentic methods across all evaluated benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[A Real-World CAD Segmentation Benchmark: Comparing Rule-Based and Learning-Based Approaches through Hole Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13466)**  `arXiv:2610.13466`  `cs.CV`  
  _Kai Schütte, Edgar Heinert, Matthias Rottmann_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  CAD segmentation datasets are predominantly synthetic or automatically labelled, leaving it unclear how well existing methods transfer to real-world engineering tasks. Fastener-relevant hole detection represents one such task, requiring human annotation and exhibiting greater geometric complexity than commonly used benchmarks. We address this gap through three main contributions. First, we develop two task-specific rule-based methods, one graph-wire-search and one multi-view based, and complement them with a human-annotated benchmark dataset derived from engineering CAD data that also enables training of UV-Net and AAGNet, representative learning-based approaches for B-Rep segmentation. Second, we conduct an extensive experimental study including parameter optimisation, evaluation on our benchmark dataset, cross-domain generalisation on a synthetic dataset, and an assessment of the feasibility of the approaches at varying dataset sizes and IoU thresholds. Third, we provide evidence-based insights into the strengths and limitations of different approaches: rule-based methods generalise well without training data, with the graph-wire-search method favouring recall and the multi-view method favouring precision, whereas learning-based methods achieve strong performance with sufficient annotations but exhibit reduced robustness under domain shift. These findings highlight trade-offs between data requirements, and robustness, and provide guidance not only for hole detection, but more broadly for real-world CAD segmentation tasks.
  </details>

- **[TextVBench: Evaluating Text Rendering and Preservation in Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13412)**  `arXiv:2610.13412`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ruiqiang Zhang, Hengyi Wang, Chang Liu, Guanjie Wang, Zehua Ma, Weiming Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Modern video generators can synthesize convincing scenes while rendering a few characters incorrectly, omitting requested text, or changing it across frames. Measuring these failures is harder than applying OCR independently to each frame: when a scene contains multiple text targets and multiple noisy OCR regions, the evaluator must first determine which observation belongs to which intended text. We introduce TextVBench, a benchmark and evaluation framework that formulates visual-text evaluation as a multi-target, multi-instance spatiotemporal correspondence problem. TextVBench contains two complementary tracks: TextVBench-T2V measures the creation and maintenance of prompt-specified text, while TextVBench-Ref2V measures preservation of text from reference images. Both tracks use target-conditioned candidate selection rather than unconstrained VLM transcription, support one target mapping to multiple OCR regions, and separate frame-level spatial fidelity from cross-frame temporal fidelity. A length-weighted harmonic score summarizes these factors, while extra-text diagnostics expose failure modes hidden by a single OCR score. The benchmark is organized around controlled text, layout, carrier, and motion attributes so that aggregate scores can be traced back to specific capability limits. Across the evaluated model runs, MiniMax-H3 reaches a T2V TextVScore of 68.32, while the strongest Ref2V score is 56.51; attribute slices reveal a pronounced drop as the number of simultaneous reference text regions increases.
  </details>

- **[ThinkDrag: Semantic Drag-Based Image Editing with Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13316)**  `arXiv:2610.13316`  `cs.CV`  
  _Armando Fortes, Tianyi Wei, Chindanai Trakantannarong, Shangchen Zhou, Xingang Pan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Drag-based image editing provides an intuitive interface for spatially controlled image manipulation by allowing users to specify handle and target points. Existing methods have made substantial progress through optimization-based, one-step, and feed-forward formulations, but they often interpret drag instructions as geometric displacements. This limits their effectiveness when the desired edit depends on interpreting image content and inferring a plausible semantic transformation from sparse point constraints. We introduce ThinkDrag, a unified multimodal framework for drag-based image editing with visual reasoning. ThinkDrag is trained to associate point constraints with meaningful object- and scene-level transformations, and can optionally produce an explicit reasoning trace that interprets the intended edit before image generation. This reasoning trace improves interpretability, particularly for ambiguous drags. Drag Endpoint Markers, motivated by an analysis of cross-image attention, provide an explicit correspondence signal that grounds each drag in image space. To support this framework, we construct a supervised dataset of semantic drag transformations paired with reasoning traces and introduce DragBench++, a benchmark targeting challenging drag-based editing scenarios with reference edit solutions. Experiments show that ThinkDrag achieves state-of-the-art performance, improving generation quality and plausibility while maintaining competitive point-following precision.
  </details>

- **[PAGE: Parts-Aware Guidance for Co-Speech Gesture Portrait Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13301)**  `arXiv:2610.13301`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yabiao Wang, Jiafu Wu, Yicheng Xu, Teng Hu, Shengzhe Zhou, Yanxiao Sun, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Co-speech gesture video generation aims to synthesize realistic, audio-driven portrait videos from a single reference image and a speech signal. Despite advances in visual quality, diffusion-based approaches still suffer from degraded local details and imperfect audio-visual alignment. We present PAGE (Parts-Aware GuidancE), a general, backbone-agnostic optimization framework for diffusion-based generation that enhances local spatial fidelity and global audio-visual consistency at both the training-objective and denoising-trajectory levels. During training, we propose Parts-Specific Reweighting (PSR), which adaptively increases supervision in semantically important regions such as lips, eyes, and hands, improving visual fidelity in perceptually critical areas. During inference, we introduce Consistency Classifier Guidance (CCG), an audio-conditioned latent classifier that identifies and corrects audio-visual inconsistencies in the generated video. We further design Differential Guidance (DG), an efficient inference strategy that refines facial and body motion separately while avoiding cross-region interference. To enable large-scale evaluation, we construct the Chinese Announcer Speech (CAS) dataset, the first comprehensive Chinese benchmark for co-speech gesture portrait video generation. Compared with the widely used PATS dataset, CAS includes substantially more identities, video clips, and scene diversity. Extensive experiments on both PATS and CAS demonstrate that PAGE achieves state-of-the-art performance in perceptual quality, gesture diversity, and audio-visual alignment. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[QCLR: A Plug-in Robustification Module via Quantization for Contrastive Learning in Self-Supervised Skeleton-Based Human Action Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13281)**  `arXiv:2610.13281`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nanzhu Chen, Feixiang He, Yunfeng Diao, Duygu Sarikaya, He Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Skeleton-based human action recognition has gained increasing attention due to the stability of skeleton data across diverse environments and advances in motion capture technologies. Following the success of supervised learning approaches, self-supervised learning approaches, particularly contrastive learning ones, have achieved impressive performance. However, they remain vulnerable to adversarial attacks. In this work, we propose a plug-in robustification module via quantization for contrastive learning in self-supervised skeleton-based human action recognition (QCLR), which enhances the robustness of representations against adversarial attacks while preserving their representational capability. Specifically, we employ a dual-level quantization scheme across the encoding pipeline, which breaks the gradient propagation and absorbs small adversarial perturbations within the quantization bins. To mitigate the impact of inevitable quantization error, we further design an attention-guided multi-faceted quantization strategy to maintain the representation quality. Experiments on several benchmark datasets demonstrate that integrating QCLR into a progressive lineage of mainstream contrastive learning models substantially improves robustness against both gradient-based white-box and gradient-free black-box adversarial attacks, while preserving highly competitive classification accuracy.
  </details>

- **[ConClick: Element-Wise Contrastive Learning Improves GUI Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13268)**  `arXiv:2610.13268`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ruotian Zhang, Yuhui Zhang, Junzhe Huang, Yu Xin, Hehe Fan, Xiaoxiao Sun_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  GUI grounding, the task of locating the interface element specified by a user instruction, is a fundamental capability of GUI agents. However, existing grounding models remain error-prone, often causing downstream task failures. We systematically evaluate 10 grounding models across five datasets and identify a major error pattern: most incorrect clicks hit non-target interface elements rather than the background, with different models repeatedly selecting the same distractors. Motivated by these findings, we propose ConClick, a contrastive learning framework that teaches models to distinguish the target element from plausible distractors. We construct ConClick-Data, a dataset containing 4,292 challenging target-distractor pairs, and train the model using complementary listwise ranking and pairwise margin objectives. ConClick improves the grounding accuracy of Qwen3-VL-8B from 54.71% to 66.03% on ScreenSpot-Pro, from 83.72% to 86.28% on MMBench-GUI L2, and from 63.33% to 70.20% on OSWorld-G, outperforming prior methods built on the same or similarly sized backbones. We further introduce ReClick, an inference-time strategy that revisits promising regions and compares predictions across competing candidates, yielding an additional 2.96 percentage-point improvement in average accuracy across the three benchmarks. Together, our analysis, dataset, and methods provide both empirical insights and practical tools for building more reliable GUI agents. Data and code are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SheafPrune: Sheaf-Guided Graph Propagation for Visual Token Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13263)**  `arXiv:2610.13263`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiaoyang Ma, Chi Wang, Hong Sun_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models process the complete visual token sequence, increasing prefill cost even when many tokens are irrelevant to the instruction. Token-wise saliency captures limited inter-token dependence; graph-based selection instead depends on how relations govern importance propagation and ranking. We study support-constrained propagation with node-degree correction. Inspired by sheaf geometry, SheafPrune uses modality-specific maps and a sparse text--visual graph to transfer relevance, then propagates scores on a separate visual graph and corrects them for degree. It retains a hard top-$k$ subset once before the frozen language model. The selector has about $4.5$M parameters and is trained end-to-end through the frozen LLaVA-NeXT-8B backbone using only the language-modeling objective. Explicit retention-ratio conditioning lets one checkpoint serve every budget. Across four backbones, ten benchmarks and seven pruning ratios, with all baselines re-implemented under one protocol, SheafPrune achieves the best retention at $22$ of $28$ backbone--budget settings, with monotonically growing margins in the moderate-to-aggressive regime. On LLaVA-NeXT, retaining $10\%$ of visual tokens yields a $5.15\times$ net prefill speedup and an $84.0\%$ end-to-end FLOPs reduction. Together with component ablations, these results support graph-constrained importance propagation and degree correction as a design for visual token selection across the evaluated backbones and tokenization schemes, improving the trade-off between accuracy retention and inference cost. code:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Beyond a Single Crop: Contextualized Embeddings for Player Re-Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13259)**  `arXiv:2610.13259`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ge Luo, Changhuo Yang, Weisi Fan, Rui Yang, Shaokai Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Person re-identification is typically addressed by learning identity-discriminative embeddings from individual person crops. However, player re-identification in broadcast sports videos presents a unique challenge: players from the same team often share similar appearances, while video sequences provide repeated observations of the same players under different viewpoints and surrounding contexts. Existing crop-based approaches largely ignore this contextual information, which can be critical for resolving visually similar identities.Here, we propose ContextReID, a contextualized embedding framework that represents a player by jointly considering other players in the same frame and observations from different frames of the broadcast sequence. By incorporating both co-occurring players and cross-frame observations, the model learns identity features relative to the surrounding visual context rather than relying only on isolated appearance cues. We implement this framework with a vision-language model that takes sets of player crops as input and generates context-aware embeddings for individual players. A combined contextual learning and retrieval objective encourages the model to capture cross-frame identity relationships while maintaining discriminative embeddings for player matching.Experiments across three multi-object tracking benchmarks show that ContextReID improves player retrieval over conventional single-crop embedding approaches in both Rank-1 and mAP. These results highlight the utility of spatial and temporal context for reliable player identification in broadcast sports videos.
  </details>

- **[Jag: Direct Box Prediction for Efficient Visual Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13239)**  `arXiv:2610.13239`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiuyuan Zhu, Ke Lu, Hao Wu, Zijin Du, Dongming Zhang, Jian Xue_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual grounding combines image--language understanding with geometric prediction, yet autoregressive multimodal models often express a box as a coordinate sequence. Sequential decoding adds computation to the multimodal processing needed for localization. Adapting these representations to box geometry offers a route to direct prediction. We present Jag, which adapts a compact pretrained multimodal model for direct continuous box prediction. A lightweight regression head reads the last valid input-token state, conditioned on the image and referring expression, and predicts all four box coordinates together. Geometric supervision jointly adapts the language backbone, visual merger, and regression head, allowing localization losses to shape both the multimodal representations and the coordinate mapping. Inference retains image--language processing and returns the box in one forward pass, without coordinate generation or text parsing. Across benchmarks, Jag consistently improves grounding accuracy over its base model across the observed target sizes and expression groups. Component ablation show that jointly adapting the representations and head substantially improves accuracy over training the head alone. Against existing specialized grounding models, it achieves competitive accuracy with lower single-request latency and GPU memory use. These results show how supervision tailored to the geometric answer can specialize a compact pretrained multimodal model for accurate grounding and efficient direct prediction. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[FPD-Drive: Future Policy Distillation for End-to-End Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13232)**  `arXiv:2610.13232`  `cs.CV`  
  _Li-Heng Chen, Qifeng Chen, Chengwei Wu, Yingjie Zhang, Sheng Yang, Hongbo Fu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  End-to-end autonomous driving models are typically trained on real-world driving logs, which record only the trajectory actually executed in each scene. This provides limited supervision for learning alternative valid behaviors. Multi-target supervision can mitigate this limitation, but broader trajectory coverage makes safe candidate selection more challenging. We propose FPD-Drive, an end-to-end planner that distills privileged future knowledge into a visual driving policy to improve both multi-trajectory supervision and safety selection. Specifically, we introduce Future-Privileged Multi-Target Distillation to transfer diverse, high-quality trajectory targets from a teacher with access to recorded future observations to the student. These targets extend supervision beyond the single recorded human trajectory. For candidate selection, we further introduce Safety-Aware Score Correction to correct ranking errors between nearby safe and unsafe candidates and raise underestimated safety scores of safe candidates. At deployment, the student requires neither future observations nor teacher inference. Our base model achieves 94.9 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 navtest and 55.9 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2 navhard. The scaled FPD-Drive-Pro improves these scores to 95.5 PDMS and 58.1 EPDMS, respectively, achieving SOTA performance among the compared methods on both benchmarks. Zero-shot closed-loop evaluation on HUGSIM further yields an HD-Score of 45.1 and RC of 55.3.
  </details>

- **[Automated Detection and Encoding of Cartographic Signs in Historical Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13224)**  `arXiv:2610.13224`  `cs.CV`  
  _Remi Petitpierre_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Symbols and icons in archival maps represent an expansive source of evidence for historical geography. In this paper, we present a two-stage framework for (1) detecting cartographic signs in historical maps and (2) generating interpretable sign embeddings that capture both semantics and stylistic variations. We also introduce CaSiDD, an open benchmark dataset spanning multiple sign categories and diverse cartographic styles, designed for generic sign detection. Using YOLOv10-X, we demonstrate the practicality of automated sign detection on historical maps. We then present strategies for creating contrasted, informative embeddings of cartographic signs. We approach embedding design systematically, maximizing expressiveness while suppressing digitization or background material confounders. Our final method, based on an adapted RADIOv4 backbone, effectively encodes both sign form and semantics, while producing interpretable representations. This work lays a foundation for scalable evidence extraction for the comparative study of map signs within cartographic culture and geohistorical research.
  </details>

- **[Destination-Conditioned Adaptation with Semantic-Geometric Alignment for Generative Inbetweening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13187)**  `arXiv:2610.13187`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhiyu Xu, Wei Shang, Yushen Xu, Wenwen Li, Zhiyan Xu, Pengfei Zhu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generative inbetweening aims to synthesize temporally coherent intermediate frames between a start frame and a target frame. Although pretrained image-to-video diffusion models provide strong priors for large-motion inbetweening, they are not explicitly designed to reach a specified target frame. They may therefore generate realistic individual frames while drifting from the target or producing unstable motion under large displacements and non-rigid dynamics. We propose DSG-GI, a framework that adapts pretrained latent video diffusion models through \textbf{D}estination-Conditioned Adaptation and \textbf{S}emantic-\textbf{G}eometric Alignment. Destination-Conditioned Adaptation treats the target frame as the destination of the sequence and provides temporally progressive guidance along the generated trajectory. Semantic-Geometric Alignment aligns the token-wise spatio-temporal relational geometry of backbone features with that of a frozen video foundation encoder and applies a flow-matching-aware latent flow constraint to samples near the data endpoint of the probability path. Together, these components strengthen destination guidance and preserve coherent semantic-geometric motion structure. Extensive experiments on standard and large-motion inbetweening benchmarks demonstrate improvements in temporal stability, target-frame faithfulness, and perceptual quality. Our code will be made publicly available.
  </details>

- **[High-Throughput Generative Video Super-Resolution on a Single Consumer GPU](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13179)**  `arXiv:2610.13179`  `cs.CV`  
  _Georgios Panaretou, Sotirios Chatzis_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generative video super-resolution (VSR) can recover visually compelling high-frequency detail by leveraging diffusion priors, but iterative sampling and heavy spatio-temporal coupling make practical deployments difficult. We study a different operating point: practical HD generative VSR on a single high-end consumer GPU without end-to-end retraining. To this end, we present a zero-shot framework built entirely from pretrained components.The proposed system decouples spatial enhancement from temporal stabilization. A time-distilled one-step diffusion backbone handles spatial upscaling, while temporal coherence is promoted by a lightweight pretrained flow-based interpolator that synthesizes intermediate frames between super-resolved keyframes. To further improve scalability, we introduce a resolution-aware hybrid attention scheme that, in a zero-shot manner, replaces selected high-resolution global-attention layers with neighborhood attention while preserving global attention in semantically critical low-resolution layers, while also improving temporal consistency.Experiments show that the proposed framework reaches up to ~32 FPS at 720p resolution under interleaved inference on a single NVIDIA RTX 4090. At 4K, the proposed attention substitution can yield up to a 44% speedup over the original attention implementation. Across standard VSR benchmarks, the method consistently shifts the speed-quality trade-off decisively toward runtime efficiency, trading reconstruction fidelity for strong no-reference perceptual quality, while remaining reasonably competitive against generative approaches in temporal consistency. It also exposes clear limitations in handling more challenging motion, which a simple motion gate partially mitigates. Code will be released upon acceptance.
  </details>

- **[Fix the Match, Not the Solver: STITCH -- a Self-Supervised Compatibility Measure for Eroded Visual Puzzles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13176)**  `arXiv:2610.13176`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nadav Alali, Ohad Ben-Shahar_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Unlike the clean and pixel-aligned complete pieces of standard jigsaw benchmarks, real-world puzzle fragments lose their boundary regions to erosion, destroying the signal that classical compatibility measures (CMs) rely on. We first re-establish, via oracle experiments, that the CM, not the solver, is the performance bottleneck: with a perfect CM, even a simple greedy solver achieves perfect reconstruction. This motivates STITCH, a self-supervised CM that sidesteps missing boundaries by comparing frozen ViT patch tokens from piece interiors via a lightweight cross-attention head (~249K trainable parameters), trained without manual annotation. Plugged into unchanged compatibility-based solvers, STITCH reaches .992/.954 Abs/Perf on JPwLEG-5 (+.629 Perf over the best prior method), nearly saturating the benchmark. On a new ImageNet 7x7 benchmark, it outperforms all alternatives, including inpaint-then-match pipelines built on modern generative models. Importantly, performance remains robust across erosion levels and generalizes to puzzles with over 40% missing pieces without retraining. Trained on the recent GAP datasets of irregular, heavily eroded fragments, the same CM recipe enables compatibility-based solvers to surpass recent learning-based solvers on GAP-3 and GAP-5. Since this leap in performance is achieved by a pluggable CM module, the takeaway is clear: fix the match, not the solver.
  </details>

- **[PreCoG: Preview-Edit for Efficient Video Correction in Generation-Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13174)**  `arXiv:2610.13174`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yilai Liu, Wenqi Pei, Zhifeng Lu, Shiyuan Zhang, Zongxi Liu, Hongyang Du_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Iterative text-to-video generation relies on feedback to correct compositional and temporal failures. However, existing methods typically require another full generation run for each correction, repeating computation and potentially disrupting already correct content. We introduce PreCoG, an iterative framework for correcting semantic failures during video generation. PreQA diagnoses failures from low-cost previews and constructs corrective conditions, while PreEdit edits cached generation trajectories under these conditions, attenuating orthogonal changes to limit disruption of already correct content. PreCoG completes generation only for the selected candidate, reusing its cached trajectory to reduce redundant computation and help preserve semantic consistency between the preview and the final video. Experiments show that PreCoG achieves the highest VBench and T2V-CompBench scores and approaches VSR's leading VS2 score at $0.56\times$ its inference time. It also outperforms GENMAC on all three quality and alignment metrics at $0.86\times$ its inference time.
  </details>

- **[From Glance to Focus: Mitigating Recognition Laziness in MLLMs for Fine-Grained Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13154)**  `arXiv:2610.13154`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yutong Yang, Zhuqi Liu, Mouxing Yang, Xi Peng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) generally fall short of expectations in Fine-Grained Visual Recognition (FGVR), although they exhibit promising FGVR foundations, i.e., visual perception and broad category knowledge. Most existing studies seek to remedy this deficiency by adopting domain-specific post-training, at the risk of compromising generalization to unseen domains. Different from these efforts, in this paper, we revisit the FGVR mechanism underlying MLLMs from both the empirical and theoretical perspectives and uncover an inference-time phenomenon termed FGVR Laziness. In brief, FGVR Laziness refers to the insufficient association between visual cues and category concepts under certain candidate category lists and recognition guidance, leaving the FGVR foundations of MLLMs largely under-utilized. To mitigate FGVR Laziness, we propose Laziness-resIstant Visual rEcognition (LIVE), a training-free framework which jointly refines candidate lists and recognition guidance to elicit the latent FGVR capability of MLLMs. Specifically, LIVE first performs query-adaptive candidate inference to derive a compact yet reliable candidate list. Meanwhile, LIVE constructs category-specific FGVR skills by mining the intra-category characteristics and inter-category differences. Finally, LIVE selectively invokes detailed skill guidance according to recognition uncertainty and category confusion, shifting MLLM-based FGVR from a cursory glance to a focus on associating discriminative visual cues with category concepts. Extensive experiments on 13 benchmarks against 24 state-of-the-art methods spanning fine-grained category recognition, action recognition, and general category recognition, verify the effectiveness and generalizability of LIVE across different MLLM backbones. The code will be released upon acceptance.
  </details>

- **[Replaying the Movie in Your Mind Decoding Dynamic Visual Perception and Recall from EEG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13120)**  `arXiv:2610.13120`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zikai Fang, Tian-Yi Zhou, Xuan-Hao Liu, Yixi Li, Xiaoyu Xing, Yutong Pei, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Decoding dynamic visual content from EEG has advanced rapidly for visual perception, yet it remains unclear which aspects of neural representations are preserved when people internally replay dynamic scenes. We introduce PAIR, an EEG benchmark collected from 20 participants using an interleaved, clip-wise paradigm that pairs naturalistic video viewing with immediate visual recall. This design enables a comparison between externally driven perception and internally generated recall for the same stimulus content within a fixed trial sequence. We benchmark multiple decoding models on hierarchical semantic classification at both fine-grained and coarse-grained levels, meta-feature decoding tasks covering motion, color, object number, face, and human presence, and a state-decoding task that discriminates perception from recall. Within-subject validation results show numerically similar performance for several model--task combinations, alongside higher perception accuracy in two coarse-grained comparisons after multiple-comparison correction. In contrast, perception and recall are highly separable at the state level under the present validation protocol. State accuracy remains high after alpha-band ablation, although this does not exclude ocular or other task-related contributions.
  </details>

- **[UniMergeTrack: Hierarchical Model Merging for General RGB-X Visual Object Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13053)**  `arXiv:2610.13053`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shiao Wang, Xiao Wang, Yuhe Ding, Jiarui Xu, Zikang Yan, Bo Jiang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  RGB-X tracking leverages complementary modalities, such as thermal infrared, event, or depth, to improve robustness under challenging conditions. Existing general-purpose RGB-X trackers typically follow two paradigms: independent task-specific training and joint training across modality combinations. However, the former suffers from knowledge isolation and model redundancy, while the latter introduces optimization interference across heterogeneous tasks. In this work, we present UniMergeTrack, a new model merging paradigm that preserves task-specific optimization while enabling effective cross-task knowledge integration. UniMergeTrack first trains modality-specific trackers independently and then performs hierarchical model merging. Specifically, transferable backbones are merged across RGB-X tasks via high-pass-filtered merging, and complemented by lightweight task-specific experts that preserve modality-specific cues. Tracking heads are merged within each task using optimal transport-guided channel alignment to mitigate hidden channel mismatch. A subsequent distribution alignment fine-tuning stage further improves compatibility between the unified backbone and task-specific heads. Extensive experiments on the LasHeR, RGBT234, VisEvent, VOT-RGBD22, COESOT, and CDTB benchmarks demonstrate that UniMergeTrack achieves state-of-the-art or competitive performance across RGB-Thermal, RGB-Event, and RGB-Depth tracking tasks, while exhibiting strong generalization to unseen datasets. The source code will be released upon acceptance. The source code of this paper will be released onthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[NOFACE3D: NOise-scheduled Focused Anchoring and Controlled Enhancement for 3D Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13050)**  `arXiv:2610.13050`  `cs.CV`  
  _James Rose, Jingwen Liang, Wenchao Ma, Oren Jacob, Haomiao Jiang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent image-to-3D models generate complete, high-quality assets from a single image, with global shape and appearance that are already convincing; what remains is the fine scale, where small, thin, elongated, or detail-rich regions are reconstructed softly and under-resolved. This gap can arise in generators with fixed-resolution spatial grids: the same spatial sampling density is used for a large, smooth, low-curvature surface and for a thin, high-curvature structure that carries most of the perceived detail. To this end, we introduce NOFACE3D, a training-free framework that refines these detail-critical regions while preserving the rest of the asset. Given a generated mesh, its input image, and a refinement box, NOFACE3D extracts the selected submesh, scales it up to fill the generator's input volume, and re-encodes it into the full-resolution latent grid. Conditioned on the image crop, it refines the region through flow-based sampling with a spatially varying noise schedule that anchors boundary latents. The refined region is then integrated back into the original asset, preserving the surrounding geometry and appearance. We implement NOFACE3D on TRELLIS.2 and its sparse voxel representation; all of it operates purely at inference time on the frozen pretrained generator. We evaluate NOFACE3D on PAniC-3D, TexVerse and BigDetail, a new benchmark of 339 assets with annotated detail-critical regions. On all three, it generates sharper geometry and finer texture detail than the baselines. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Redirecting Teacher Supervision: Temporal-Response-Guided Latent Distillation for Video-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13045)**  `arXiv:2610.13045`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xinkai Lu, Junli Zha, Haolan Wei, Jiahui Wang, Chao Zhou, Xiang Song_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  On-policy distillation (OPD) transfers capabilities through teacher predictions on student-generated sequences, as studied by GKD and MiniLLM for language models and Video-OPD for video tasks. We ask how internal supervision can further support temporal learning. Direct hidden-state alignment does not explicitly use the student's temporal responses to choose a supervision direction. We propose Temporal-Response Guidance (TRG), which measures representation responses to local temporal perturbations, adds the teacher correction's projection onto their span back to the original correction, and restores its original norm. The projection is not normalized separately, and correction components outside the response space are retained. With an orthogonal tangent projector and a detached target, TRG changes the supervision direction while preserving the cosine-loss value and aligned-representation gradient norm at target construction. On TempCompass, Video-MME, MVBench, and Video-MMMU, TRG improves the 4B student's macro accuracy by 2.86 percentage points over the reported output-only OPD baseline.
  </details>

- **[Unified Generative Video Compression with Autoregressive and Bidirectional Video Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13023)**  `arXiv:2610.13023`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tianyu Zhang, Shuai Huo, Dengchao Jin, Hewei Liu, Meng Lei, Tianwen Wu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video diffusion models can reconstruct visually compelling content from severely compressed representations at ultra-low bitrates, but they impose a rigid deployment trade-off. Bidirectional (BI) models achieve high quality through dense full-sequence attention, yet require the entire sequence before reconstruction and incur substantial latency.
